## management-governance — module synthesis

# Management-Governance Module — UBER (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Uber's stewardship record is genuinely mixed: neither a clean pass nor a governance failure. Dara Khosrowshahi delivered a proven, numbers-backed turnaround — operating income rose from a $(1,832)mm loss in FY2022 to $5,565mm in FY2025 — and the board is genuinely independent, with 90% independent directors, no poison pill, no dual-class stock, and zero disclosed related-party transactions above the $120,000 threshold. Capital allocation is capped at 50 of 100, though, by a serial-acquirer pattern that culminated in the $14.8bn, debt-funded Delivery Hero deal, funded partly by pulling $4bn from a promised buyback program. Insiders hold just 0.18% of shares, almost entirely stock-compensation-derived rather than bought, and no compensation-plan disclosure could be verified to confirm what pay actually rewards. Verdict: standard, mixed stewardship.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict: Standard / mixed** — conventional-to-strong governance mechanics (board, related-party discipline) offset by an unproven incentive design, thin insider skin-in-the-game, and a capital-allocation program capped by a serial-acquirer red flag. Neither a clear positive nor a clear negative on the whole record.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found no auditor qualification, no going-concern note, no promoter pledge (no promoter exists), no related-party transaction >25% of revenue, no auditor change, no material restatement (the Capital IQ "RC" tag on FY2023/FY2024 columns is a vendor reclassification code, not a company-disclosed restatement), and no active enforcement action. Verdict-lock deference: **not triggered.**
- Management quality /100: **65**
- Capital allocation /100: **50** *(capped — see §4)*
- Incentive alignment /100: **10** *(capped max 50 — see §4; unproven, not disproven)*
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **74** *(blended: 65% weight on `05` Board & Shareholder Rights Score of 84, 35% weight on `04` Ownership Alignment Score of 55 — see §3)*
- Disclosure candor /100: **56**
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = worse, inverted)*: **65**
- Data quality /100: **62** *(from `00`; Partial sufficiency verdict, upgraded modestly by `05`'s primary DEF 14A pull but capped by the compensation gap and thin transcript history)*
- Overall usefulness /100: **68** *(capped max 70 by the no-compensation-disclosure rule)*
- Insider ownership (one line): Officers and directors together hold **0.18%** of shares (3,586,107 shares, $256.8mm), almost entirely RSU-vest-derived, not cash-bought; net insider activity over the trailing 6 months is modest net selling (−33,973 shares) that is almost entirely mechanical sell-to-cover, not opportunistic dumping [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`].
- Biggest governance signal (one line): A genuinely independent, well-run board (90% independent, no poison pill, zero disclosed RPT) sits above a capital-allocation program that has pivoted hard toward debt-funded M&A — at least seven deals in ~18 months, culminating in the $14.8bn Delivery Hero acquisition financed by a ~€14bn bridge facility and funded in part by diverting $4bn from the company's own promised buyback framework.
- **Governance Score /100** — `0.20×50 + 0.18×10 + 0.18×74 + 0.16×56 + 0.16×65 + 0.12×(100−65)` = `10 + 1.80 + 13.32 + 8.96 + 10.40 + 4.20` = **48.68 ≈ 49**
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100** (= 49 × 65/100) = **31.85 ≈ 32**
- **Governance Rating: Weak** *(40–54 band; not from a forced override — see the Critical-flag reconciliation note below)*
- **Confidence Score /100: 65** *(reflects a genuine mix: Tier-1 primary-filing evidence for the board/RPT/shareholder-rights read via `05`'s SEC EDGAR pull, versus vendor-tier or fully-missing evidence for compensation, and a single-quarter transcript for candor)*
- **Red-Flag Count: 2 distinct IDs (7 firing rows) / Critical Red-Flag Count: 0** *(for the purposes of the module's hard "no better than Weak / Serious governance concerns" override — see reconciliation note immediately below; RF-CAP-004 carries "Critical" **materiality** in its own findings rows and drives its own score caps, but is not one of the enumerated fraud / going-concern / enforcement / restatement / RPT-leakage->10% triggers that MODULE_RULES.md's Red-Flag Trigger Engine defines as forcing the universal rating/verdict lock)*

**Note on the Critical-flag interpretation.** MODULE_RULES.md states in two places that a "Critical red flag" forces the rating to no better than "Weak" and the verdict to no better than "Serious governance concerns." The Red-Flag Trigger Engine section enumerates what that specific, harder tier of "Critical" means: "fraud allegation, going concern, enforcement, restatement, RPT leakage >10%." RF-CAP-004 (the serial-acquirer / Delivery Hero pattern) is separately defined in the §24 Filter 4 score-cap row as its own conviction cap (Capital allocation max 50; Governance risk floor 60) — a penalty, not a hard lock. Applying the conservative default where the two provisions could be read either way, this synthesis treats RF-CAP-004 as triggering its own explicit caps (both applied below) without invoking the universal "Serious governance concerns" lock, consistent with CLAUDE.md §24's framing that the six rejector filters are "not new hard disqualifiers... they define... a score penalty and a conviction cap," not a second disqualifier-scan. The Governance Score (49) independently lands in the "Weak" band on its own arithmetic, so the rating outcome is the same either way — this note exists so the master synthesizer understands why "Serious governance concerns" was NOT selected as the verdict despite a Critical-materiality finding being present.

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** Capital allocation is the dominant, least-proven, highest-risk decision this management team has made — at least seven acquisitions in ~18 months culminating in a $14.8bn, debt-funded Delivery Hero deal (10.6% of Uber's own market cap), financed by a fresh ~€14bn bridge facility, requiring a forced $1.6bn divestiture to clear antitrust, and funded in part by pulling $4bn from a capital-return promise made to shareholders [`02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`, RF-CAP-004]. Net debt jumped from $76mm to $9,340mm in a single LTM window, before the much larger bridge facility is even drawn [02-014].
- **Strongest bull point (steelman):** The organic business is a genuine per-share value creator run by a management team with a proven, delivered (not promised) turnaround — operating income rose from a $(1,832)mm loss (FY2022) to $5,565mm (FY2025) on capex that never exceeded $336mm/year, an incremental ROIC in the 25–35% range [`02`, §4] — sitting under a board that is independently strong by every checkable metric (90% independent, fully independent Audit/Comp/Nominating committees, no poison pill, no dual-class stock, zero disclosed related-party transactions above $120,000) [`05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`]. On the two metrics management actually guides (Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EPS), guidance has been met or beaten in each of the last several quarters [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, §4].
- **Single killer risk:** The Delivery Hero deal does not close until H2 2027 and its $1.2bn run-rate synergy target is a management-communicated goal, not a delivered result [`02`, §2]. If integration disappoints or leverage overshoots the (web-sourced, unverified) <2x gross-leverage target while the ~$10bn multi-year AV investment program is running concurrently, this management team — which has already shown it will override its own capital-return promise under M&A pressure — has both the balance sheet capacity and the demonstrated willingness to keep doing it again.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** Buybacks over FY2024–LTM Jun-2026 ran ~3.9x stock-based compensation in dollar terms and did reduce net share count (−2.2%), clearing the minimum "not merely offsetting dilution" bar [02-009, 02-011] — i.e., capital allocation is not uniformly poor, only the M&A component is capped. Say-on-Pay support has averaged ~91% over the past five years with no year below ~85% [`05`, 05-018], a real (if incomplete, proxy-advisor-recommendation-blind) signal that the shareholder base that CAN see the actual pay structure is not objecting to it.

## 3. Reconciliation

**Disagreement 1 — does a primary proxy statement exist in the accessible record?** `00_governance-data-triage.md`, `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`, `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, and `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md` all state flatly that no DEF 14A exists anywhere in `data/UBER/` and score/cap accordingly (incentive alignment capped at 10/100 of a possible 50; compensation and say-on-pay marked "Insufficient Data"). `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md` disagrees in practice: it retrieved Uber's actual FY2026 DEF 14A directly from SEC EDGAR (filed 2026-03-23, cited with a direct URL) and used it as its primary Tier-1 source for board composition, related-party transactions, takeover defenses, and — notably — Say-on-Pay history (91% average support over five years). **Reconciled view:** this is not a genuine data non-existence but an inter-agent execution gap — the document exists and is retrievable, but `03` (whose specific job is compensation) did not pull it, so its "Insufficient Data" verdict on CEO/CFO pay mix, bonus/LTIP metrics and weights stands as reported (this synthesis does not re-run `03`'s work), while `06`'s and `04`'s "Insufficient Data" findings on RPT and say-on-pay are **superseded** by `05`'s primary-filing findings (zero material RPT disclosed above $120,000; 91% average Say-on-Pay support). The conservative, evidence-based resolution: treat `05`'s primary-sourced facts as authoritative wherever they overlap with another agent's "Insufficient Data" claim (per CLAUDE.md §4, a filing beats an absence-of-evidence claim), but do **not** assume the DEF 14A's CD&A (compensation metrics and weights) has actually been read and verified — `05` did not extract that section, so incentive-alignment remains genuinely unproven, not merely under-sourced. This is the single most actionable finding in this synthesis: the primary source almost certainly needed to close the compensation gap has already been located.

**Disagreement 2 — degree of capital-allocation credit.** `01_management-and-track-record.md` reads the buyback-framework override ($4bn redirected to the Delivery Hero stake) as "Mixed" — a promise whose delivery changed direction, but explained candidly (see `06`). `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md` treats the same fact set more severely, since its mandate is the capital-allocation record itself, not the communication around it, and applies the RF-CAP-004 cap. No true contradiction — different agents judging different dimensions of the same fact — but the more conservative, capital-allocation-specific verdict (Capital allocation capped at 50/100) is carried forward as authoritative for the composite score, consistent with CLAUDE.md's conservative-default rule.

**No other material disagreements.** All specialists agree independently that: no promoter/pledge structure exists, no dual-class stock exists, no controlling owner exists (RF-OWN-004 not triggered), no unresolved adverse integrity signal exists (RF-MGT-005 not triggered), and the historical Khosrowshahi-era turnaround clears the §24 Filter 2 delivered-inflection bar (RF-MGT-004 not triggered).

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Judged on delivery, not narrative: Dara Khosrowshahi's management team has kept its promises on the two metrics it actually guides — Adjusted EBITDA and Adjusted EPS have been met or beaten in each of the last several quarters — and the organic business has created real per-share value, turning a $(1,832)mm operating loss (FY2022) into $5,565mm of operating income (FY2025) on capex that never topped $336mm a year, an incremental return on capital in the 25–35% range. But capital allocation as a whole is capped at 50/100 because the M&A program — at least seven deals in eighteen months, capped by the $14.8bn debt-funded Delivery Hero acquisition — is the single largest, least-proven, highest-risk capital decision on the books, financed in part by pulling $4bn from a buyback promise made to shareholders. Incentives cannot be verified to point management at per-share value rather than size, because no compensation-metric disclosure was actually read by this module (even though the underlying DEF 14A has now been located by `05`), and insiders hold just 0.18% of the company — almost entirely stock-compensation-derived, not bought with personal cash — so there is little skin in the game backing the promises. Minority shareholders are well protected on paper (a 90%-independent board, no poison pill, no dual-class stock, zero disclosed related-party transactions above $120,000) but have one real procedural gap: no right to act by written consent, and a 25%-of-voting-power bar to call a special meeting that no single holder is close to clearing alone. The single most important reason to trust this team with more shareholder capital is the proven, numbers-backed turnaround and a genuinely independent board; the single most important reason not to hand them more without watching closely is that the same team has just shown it will override its own capital-return promise to chase a transformative, debt-funded acquisition it cannot yet prove will work.

## 5B. Peer Governance Benchmark

No peer set — relative governance not assessed. Every specialist in this module (`01` through `06`) independently reported "No peer set — relative governance not assessed" against every finding row. `business-model/08_competitive-map.md` exists and identifies Uber's competitive peer set for business-model purposes, but no Layer-2 specialist in this module used it to benchmark governance metrics (board independence %, insider/promoter holding %, pledge %, non-audit/audit fee ratio, RPT intensity, or AGM votes-against) against named peers. This is a genuine gap for a future run, not a finding that peers are unavailable in principle — Lyft and DoorDash proxy data were used in `03` for compensation-design texture only (web-sourced, unverified), not as a scored peer benchmark.

No prior run — first governance snapshot. No prior dated run exists for UBER under `analyses/UBER_{prior-date}/management-governance/`; there is nothing to compare against. All findings above should be treated as the baseline for future delta tracking (board/KMP changes, promoter holding/pledge, RPT intensity, new legal/regulatory items, new AGM opposition, CFO/PAT).

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

- **CFO turnover (01-002, 01-014):** Is the ~2-year tenure of the prior CFO (Mahendra-Rajah) genuinely unrelated to financial-reporting matters, as web-sourced (unverified) reports claim? Confirm via the next 8-K or proxy. Does the pattern (3 CFOs since 2018) affect execution risk specifically around Delivery Hero financing and AV capital deployment, where institutional memory matters most?
- **Serial-acquirer / Delivery Hero (02-004, 02-005, 02-006, 02-014, RF-CAP-004):** Is the ~$1.2bn run-rate synergy target (management-communicated, not itemized in the pool) credible given no closed integration exists yet? Is this company-specific execution risk or a sector-wide consolidation wave (Delivery Hero, DoorDash, and peers are all pursuing M&A)? Does the leverage trajectory stay under the (unverified) <2x gross-leverage target through close (H2 2027)? Does the buyback program actually resume on the "months, not quarters" timeline management stated?
- **Compensation disclosure gap (03-001 through 03-008):** Now that `05` has located the primary FY2026 DEF 14A on SEC EDGAR, does its Compensation Discussion & Analysis and Summary Compensation Table show per-share/returns-based metrics (ROIC, EPS, TSR) or size-based metrics (revenue, Gross Bookings, deal count) for the CEO and CFO bonus/LTIP plans? This is directly answerable from a document this module has already located but not yet read for this purpose.
- **PIF-affiliated Audit Committee seat (05-005):** Has the Audit Committee handled any PIF-adjacent commercial or investment matter since Alnowaiser's Nov-2023 appointment? Is there any commercial relationship between Uber and PIF portfolio companies that would create a disclosable conflict beyond the beneficial-ownership disclosure already made?
- **No written-consent right (05-016):** Does Uber's actual Certificate of Incorporation (not independently re-read in full by `05`) confirm the absence of a written-consent right? Is the 25%-of-voting-power special-meeting threshold a genuine constraint given no single holder exceeds ~9.3%?
- **Non-GAAP itemization and deferred-tax releases (06-004, 06-005, RF-DISC-002):** Is the unitemized ~25% of Adjusted EBITDA's addback material to a normalized-earnings view of the business? Will a third consecutive deferred-tax valuation-allowance release occur in FY2026, and if so, does management address it proactively on a future call rather than leaving it to be found by cross-referencing the balance sheet?
- **Insider ownership quality (04-005):** Would a primary Form 4 filing (once available) confirm that zero of the trailing-6-month "purchase" transactions were genuine open-market cash buys, or does the Feb-24-2026 CFO transaction ($1.6mm) turn out to be a discretionary purchase rather than an option exercise?

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Stewardship verdict:** Standard / mixed. Strongest evidence for it: a proven, delivered turnaround (operating income $(1,832)mm FY2022 → $5,565mm FY2025) sitting under a genuinely independent board (90% independent, zero disclosed RPT above $120,000), offset by a capital-allocation program capped at 50/100 for a serial-acquirer pattern and an incentive-alignment read that is unproven (not disproven) for lack of a read compensation disclosure.
- **Capital-allocation record:** organic business is value-creative (incremental ROIC ~25–35%, capex-light); buybacks clear the minimum bar (net share count −2.2% since Dec-2024, ~3.9x stock-based comp in dollar terms) but were not price-disciplined on the observable trading range; the M&A program — ≥7 deals in ~18 months culminating in the $14.8bn, debt-funded Delivery Hero acquisition — is the dominant, least-proven, highest-risk capital decision and caps the overall score. RF-CAP-004 triggered.
- **Incentive/ownership alignment:** cannot be confirmed to point management at per-share value vs. size — no compensation-metric disclosure was actually read in this module (score 10/100, capped max 50). Insiders hold 0.18% of shares, almost entirely stock-comp-derived, not bought; net insider activity is modest selling that is almost entirely mechanical sell-to-cover, not opportunistic. No pledging, no dual-class stock, no controlling owner (RF-OWN-004 not triggered).
- **Biggest governance risk / red flag:** RF-CAP-004 (serial-acquirer / Delivery Hero pattern) — Critical materiality, caps Capital allocation at 50/100 and floors Governance risk at 60 (set at 65 here). Secondary: RF-DISC-002 (High) — a persistently unitemized ~25% of Adjusted EBITDA's addback and a $10.1bn combined two-year deferred-tax GAAP-inflation, neither proactively flagged by management.
- **Hard disqualifier:** None. `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` verbatim: "No disqualifier fact found in the available data pool," with the caveat that related-party, restatement, and enforcement rows rest on absence-of-evidence in a vendor-export/transcript-only pool rather than a primary filing's clean attestation (partially resolved for related-party by `05`'s primary DEF 14A pull — zero material RPT above $120,000 disclosed).
- **§24 rejector filters that tripped:** Filter 4 (serial acquirer, RF-CAP-004) — capped Capital allocation at 50, floored Governance risk at 60. Filters 1 (integrity), 2 (turnaround), 3 (leverage — owned by balance-sheet-survival, not this module), and 6 (unaligned owner) did NOT trip: no adverse integrity signal, the historical turnaround already cleared the ≥2–3-year delivered-inflection bar, and no controlling owner exists.
- **Partial-data caps applied:** Incentive alignment capped max 50 (scored 10) and Overall usefulness capped max 70 (scored 68) — no compensation-metric disclosure was read. Disclosure candor's "no prior transcripts" cap (max 65) was not binding since the raw score (56) already sits below it.
- **Biggest missing data point — reframed:** NOT "obtain the DEF 14A" — `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md` already retrieved Uber's FY2026 DEF 14A from SEC EDGAR and used it for board, related-party, and shareholder-rights findings. The actual highest-value next step is having the incentives-and-compensation specialist (`03`) read the Compensation Discussion & Analysis and Summary Compensation Table sections of that SAME document, which it did not use. This would very likely resolve the single largest remaining score cap in this module (Incentive alignment, currently 10/100 of a possible 50).
- **Explicit handoff:** this module supersedes the `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` quick-read; the master synthesizer should treat this module's governance verdict and scores as the primary governance read.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management kept its word on the two numbers it actually promises (Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EPS) — beaten or met the last several quarters running.
- Capital allocation is a mixed bag: the core business turns cash into profit efficiently, but the acquisition binge — 7+ deals in 18 months, capped by a $14.8bn debt-funded purchase of Delivery Hero — is the riskiest, least-proven call management has made, and it broke its own buyback promise to help pay for it.
- What the pay actually rewards is unknown: no proxy's pay-plan section was read for this report, so we cannot say if bonuses reward per-share value or just growing bigger.
- Insiders barely own the stock — 0.18% combined, almost all from stock grants vesting, not personal cash purchases — and they've been net sellers (mechanically, not opportunistically) over the last six months.
- Minority shareholders are protected on paper: an independent board, no poison pill, no dual-class shares, and zero disclosed related-party deals above $120,000 — but they can't force a vote between annual meetings without a coalition holding 25% of the votes.
- Management is mostly candid when things go wrong on the one call available — it owned the buyback pause and an accounting optics issue with real numbers — but roughly a quarter of its own "adjusted profit" add-back is never explained, and a $10 billion two-year tax-related profit boost was never flagged to investors.
- No hard disqualifier (no fraud, no going-concern issue, no pledged shares, no rigged books) was found.
- This module is useful for the master synthesizer, with one flagged gap: the compensation-design question is still open even though the document to answer it has already been located.

```red_flags.csv
red_flag_id,trigger,severity,evidence,source_and_date,score_impact,follow_up
RF-CAP-004,"Serial-acquirer pattern: >=7 acquisitions in ~18 months, culminating in the $14.8bn debt-funded Delivery Hero deal (10.6% of Uber's own market cap), financed by a ~EUR14bn bridge facility, requiring a forced $1.6bn divestiture, net debt jumping from $76mm to $9340mm in one LTM window","Critical (materiality); conviction cap, not a hard verdict lock","Delivery Hero SE deal entry ($14.8bn equity value, agreed 2026-07-16); >=7 deals incl. Trendyol Go, Careem reconsolidation, Getir, SS Ventures, Blacklane, SpotHero, Segments.ai, Crown Taxi","Capital IQ M&A database (CIQReportLandscape.rtf); Public Company Profile.rtf; Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, 2026-08-05","Capital allocation capped at 50/100 (from raw 58); Governance risk floored at 60, set at 65","Track gross leverage vs the (web-sourced, unverified) <2x target through deal close (H2 2027); re-score capital allocation once integration results are observable"
RF-DISC-002,"Recurring one-off / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs: ~25% of Adjusted EBITDA's own addback never itemized (FY2025 gap +38.3% vs GAAP-based EBITDA); a deferred-tax valuation-allowance release inflated GAAP net income by a combined $10104mm over two consecutive years (FY2024 $5758mm + FY2025 $4346mm), never flagged as a distortion on any call in the pool",High,"Adj. EBITDA $8730mm vs GAAP-based EBITDA $6312mm (FY2025); deferred-tax releases per earnings/06_earnings-quality.md §4,5,7,8","earnings/06_earnings-quality.md; business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md, 2026-08-06","Disclosure candor: -6 (06-004), -6 (06-005) against a raw total of 56/100","Request an itemized non-GAAP reconciliation; confirm via the next annual filing whether a third consecutive deferred-tax release occurs"
```

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Partial
- **Reason:** Ownership/insider data, board composition, and a 5-year capital-allocation history are all usable (via Capital IQ vendor exports, not primary filings), but compensation disclosure (metrics/weights) and related-party disclosure are entirely absent from the pool, and no proxy/DEF 14A, 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, or shareholder letter exists anywhere in `data/UBER/`.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record (01, thin on multi-quarter transcript history but usable via guidance/surprise history + one transcript), capital allocation (02, full 5-year history), ownership (04, via CIQ vendor ownership/insider tables), board (05, composition/tenure usable; related-party sub-question not assessable), candor (06, usable via guidance-vs-actual and non-GAAP read, thin on multi-quarter transcript tone). Incentives (03) is the most constrained — no compensation structure data exists in this pool at all.
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — "No disqualifier fact found in the available data pool," with an explicit caveat that rows for related-party transactions, restatement, and enforcement rest on absence-of-evidence in a vendor-export/transcript-only dataset, not a primary filing's clean attestation. Separately, `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` flagged a §24 Filter 4 serial-acquirer pattern (Delivery Hero, $14.8bn, ~€14bn debt-funded, ~10% of Uber's market value, plus 7+ bolt-ons in ~18 months) that caps THAT agent's Capital Allocation Score at 50/100 — this is a score cap under MODULE_RULES.md, not a hard disqualifier, and this module's `02_capital-allocation-scorecard` should apply the same RF-CAP-004 cap logic rather than re-litigate the underlying fact.
- **Active partial-data caps:**
  - No proxy / compensation disclosure → Incentive alignment max 50; Overall usefulness max 70 (agents 03, 99)
  - No related-party disclosure anywhere in the pool → board/shareholder-rights read (05) cannot assess related-party leakage; treat as "Insufficient Data," not "clean," per MODULE_RULES's conservative default
  - Ownership/insider data present only at Capital IQ vendor tier (confidence 3, not a filed 13D/13G/Form 4) → lower confidence on ownership/insider findings even though no hard score cap applies
  - Board composition present only via vendor profile, no formal proxy confirming committee charters or an explicit independence % → lower confidence on board findings
  - Only one quarter's transcript, no shareholder letter → promise-vs-delivery and candor reads (01, 06) limited to filings/guidance history plus one transcript, per the standard cap
- **Critical missing items:**
  - Compensation disclosure (CD&A / LTIP metrics and weights) — fully absent
  - Related-party transaction disclosure — fully absent
  - Any primary SEC filing (DEF 14A, 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, Form 4, Schedule 13D/13G) — none in the pool
- **Single highest-value missing document:** DEF 14A (proxy statement) — it would simultaneously fill the compensation-disclosure gap, confirm board independence/committee structure with a filed source, and disclose related-party transactions, closing all three critical gaps at once.

## management-governance / 01_management-and-track-record.md

_Source: `01_management-and-track-record.md`_

# Management & Track Record — UBER

**Data-pool caveat (carried from `00_governance-data-triage.md`):** no DEF 14A, 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, or shareholder letter is present anywhere in `data/UBER/`. Executive tenure and bios come from Capital IQ's *Professionals.rtf* / *Board Members.rtf* profiles (vendor-tier, confidence 3), the promise-vs-delivery read comes from Capital IQ's Guidance/Consensus/Surprise workbook tabs (vendor-parsed but filing/press-release-anchored, confidence 3) plus one earnings-call transcript (Q2 FY2026, Aug-05-2026), and one CFO-departure fact is filled from dated web sources because the pool has no 8-K to confirm it. This is a **thin, not absent**, track-record dataset: multiple quarters of guidance-vs-actual history exist (2019–2026), but there is no shareholder letter and only one quarter's transcript, so tone/candor evidence is limited. Capped accordingly — see §6.

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO | Dara Khosrowshahi | September 2017 | ~8 years 11 months (as of Aug-2026) | Former CEO & President, Expedia Group (2005–2017); former CFO, IAC/InterActiveCorp and HSN, Inc.; joined Uber after the 2017 Travis Kalanick-era governance and culture crisis | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf |
| CFO | Balaji Krishnamurthy, CFA | February 16, 2026 | ~6 months | Internal promotion — Head of Investor Relations at Uber (2020–2023), VP Strategic Finance & IR (2023–2026), then CFO; prior to Uber, Associate at Goldman Sachs Research (to Nov-2019) | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf |
| President & COO | Andrew G. MacDonald | Named President/COO (from prior SVP Global Mobility role, 2019–2025) | ~14 years at Uber (joined 2012) | Ran Global Mobility, various regional GM roles 2012–2019; ex-Bain & Company management consultant | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf |
| Board Chair (Independent) | Ronald D. Sugar, Ph.D. | August 2018 | ~8 years | Former Chairman & CEO, Northrop Grumman (2003–2009); sitting independent director, Apple Inc. | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Board Members.rtf, "Years on Board: 2018-Present" |
| Co-Founder / Board Observer | Garrett Camp | Board Observer & Product Advisor since May-2020 (director until then) | ~16 years since founding (2010) | Original co-founder and first-version designer of Uber; no executive or CEO role since 2017 | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Board Members.rtf |

**Founder / owner-operator status:** the CEO is **not a founder**. Khosrowshahi was hired from outside in September 2017, after the board removed founder-CEO Travis Kalanick amid a governance and workplace-culture crisis [Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf]. Co-founder Garrett Camp remains only a non-voting Board Observer with no operating role [Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Board Members.rtf]. Individual/insider ownership across all officers and directors combined is ~0.18% of shares outstanding [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, citing UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf] — Uber is professionally managed, not owner-operated, and no executive holds a meaningful equity stake bought (rather than granted). This is Not proven from available data to be an "owner-operator" structure by any definition this module uses.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | **CFO:** Prashanth Mahendra-Rajah stepped down effective Feb-16-2026 after ~27 months in the role (CFO since early 2024), succeeded by internal promotion Balaji Krishnamurthy. Mahendra-Rajah stated on the departure call he was leaving "for another opportunity ... to serve America," and Uber stated the change was **not** due to a disagreement over financial disclosures or accounting matters — this specific characterization is **web-sourced, unverified**, since no 8-K or company transcript in the pool covers the departure call itself. **CTO:** Praveen Neppalli Naga became full CTO in December 2025, having previously been CTO for Mobility & Delivery only since Nov-2024; the pool does not name or date-stamp his predecessor as full-company CTO. | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf (Krishnamurthy, Naga bios); Web: TechCrunch, "Uber appoints new CFO as its AV plans accelerate," 2026-02-04; Web: CFO Dive, "Uber taps insider for CFO amid robotaxi push," 2026 (unverified, dated) |
| CFO turnover | Three different people have held the CFO title since 2018: Nelson Chai (to ~Dec-2023, per `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` and multiple dated web sources — not independently confirmed in this pool's Professionals.rtf, which lists only current/recent officers), Prashanth Mahendra-Rajah (~Jan/Feb-2024 to Feb-2026, ~2 years), and Balaji Krishnamurthy (Feb-2026–present). Mahendra-Rajah's ~2-year tenure is short relative to a CFO's typical multi-year cycle at a company this size. **This is a genuine stability warning**, though the successor is a long-tenured internal Uber finance executive (6 years in the finance/IR function before becoming CFO), which mitigates — but does not erase — the continuity risk. | `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (Signal Table, "Senior management turnover" row, severity 48/100); Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf |
| CEO succession / continuity | No CEO change. Khosrowshahi has run Uber continuously since Sep-2017 (~9 years) and Board Chair Sugar since Aug-2018 (~8 years) — both stable throughout the CFO/CTO churn described above. | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf; Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Board Members.rtf |

**Flag:** CFO turnover (3 people in ~8 years, with the middle tenure unusually short at ~2 years) is a monitorable stability warning. It is not, on the evidence available, an integrity or accounting-dispute signal — the departure is characterized (web-sourced, unverified) as voluntary and unrelated to financial reporting — but repeated CFO transitions raise execution and institutional-memory risk around a period (Delivery Hero financing, AV capital deployment) when finance leadership continuity matters most.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Management describes Uber as a three-segment (Mobility, Delivery, Freight) marketplace pursuing broad-based volume growth — penetrating "sparse markets" where under 10% of eligible U.S. consumers used Uber in the past year, versus over 50% in dense markets — while layering in a fourth, not-yet-monetized bet: positioning Uber as the "commercialization platform" for autonomous-vehicle (AV) makers, funded by roughly $10 billion of equity investments and vehicle-purchase commitments (120,000 vehicles) over a multi-year period, mostly through partner stakes (Waymo, Wayve, Zoox, Nuro, Pony, Baidu) rather than building Uber's own self-driving technology [Q2 FY2026 earnings call transcript, prepared remarks and Q&A, Aug-05-2026]. The explicit, checkable targets on the table: Adjusted EBITDA and Adjusted EPS guided quarter by quarter (Revenue has not been guided since FQ2 2020); a stated capital-return framework of "about 50% of free cash flow" to buybacks [Q2 FY2026 earnings call transcript, CFO remarks]; and a qualitative claim of "a track record of being disciplined on headcount addition over multiple years," offered as the basis for expecting AI-driven productivity gains to moderate future headcount growth [Q2 FY2026 earnings call transcript, CFO remarks, p.9] — this headcount claim has no supporting time series in the data pool and is **not verifiable from available data**.

| "About 50% of free cash flow" returned via buybacks | Standing capital-return framework | Buybacks ran materially above 50% of trailing FCF through most of FY2024–FY2025 (~$6.5–6.9bn/yr against ~$10bn LTM FCF), then were **throttled** — $4bn of capital was pulled from buybacks in Q2 2026 to fund a pre-emptive Delivery Hero share stake ahead of the announced acquisition | **Mixed** — the long-run average has exceeded the stated 50%, but the framework was overridden without a stated pause target when a large M&A opportunity arose; this is a capital-allocation-scorecard question in the first instance (see `02_capital-allocation-scorecard`), noted here because it is a specific prior promise whose delivery changed direction | `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (Signal Table, "Net share count trajectory" and "Acquisition pattern" rows); Q2 FY2026 earnings call transcript, CFO remarks, p.12 |

**Read across the record:** on the two metrics management actually guides (Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EPS), the company has met or beaten its own guidance in each of the last several quarters shown in the pool, and the multi-year Guidance tab (2019–2026) shows a long run of "as expected" or beaten Adjusted EBITDA prints, with only isolated early-history misses (e.g., FQ1 2019 EBITDA guidance $(954)mm–$(847)mm vs. actual $(869)mm, a small miss inside the range direction) [Capital IQ Estimates Report, Guidance tab, "EBITDA" row, FQ1 2019–FQ3 2026]. The company stopped guiding the metric now under the most pressure — Revenue, missed by the Street's own estimate twice running — which is a **choice about what to promise**, not a broken promise, but it narrows what can be independently checked on the top line.

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | **Yes, historically** — Khosrowshahi was brought in from outside in Sep-2017 specifically to fix a company in a governance, culture, and regulatory crisis under founder-CEO Travis Kalanick. That turnaround is **not the live thesis today**; it is a completed, multi-year fact pattern that should be judged on results, not re-litigated as a pitch. | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Professionals.rtf |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered (not promised) | Operating Income rose from $1,110mm (FY2023) to $2,799mm (FY2024) to $5,565mm (FY2025) — three consecutive years of growing, positive operating income, alongside cash from operations rising from $642mm (FY2022) to $3,585mm (FY2023) to $7,137mm (FY2024) to $10,099mm (FY2025) [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`]. This clears the §24 Filter 2 bar of ≥2–3 years of *delivered* (not promised) operating improvement — the Khosrowshahi-era turnaround is proven, not a résumé-and-plan pitch. | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Financials.xls, Income Statement / Cash Flow tabs, FY2022–FY2025 |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? (not evidence) | No — the operating-income and cash-flow trend above is the evidence, not the CEO's Expedia résumé or a strategy deck. The current forward-looking bet (the AV "commercialization platform" strategy, ~$10bn multi-year spend) **is** closer to a plan-not-yet-proven, and should be judged by the §24 Filter 2 standard going forward, not credited in advance. | Q2 FY2026 earnings call transcript |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | **None routed.** The disqualifier scan found no adverse integrity signal, no auditor qualification, no going-concern flag, and no fraud or enforcement allegation in the available pool; RF-MGT-005 is **not** triggered. | `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, §3 ("No disqualifier fact found in the available data pool") |

**Turnaround rule applied:** because the historical turnaround already clears the ≥2–3-year delivered-inflection bar, no base-rate penalty or RF-MGT-004 applies to *that* history. The live forward bet — AV commercialization — is a genuinely new, unproven plan layered on top of a mature, already-turned-around core business; it should be tracked with the same skepticism the turnaround rule demands of any new plan, not given credit from the earlier, already-proven turnaround.

**Integrity rule applied:** no unresolved adverse integrity signal exists in this data pool to chase. No RF-MGT-005 line is warranted.

## 5. Management Read

Dara Khosrowshahi has run Uber for nearly nine years and delivered a real, numbers-backed turnaround — operating income and operating cash flow both grew for three straight years (FY2023–FY2025) — which is proof, not a pitch [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`]. On the two metrics this management team actually guides, Adjusted EBITDA and Adjusted EPS, the record over the last several quarters is one of promises kept or beaten, though the company stopped guiding Revenue back in 2020 and that is exactly the line now missing Street estimates two quarters running. The single biggest management signal is the CFO chair: three different people have held it since 2018, with the middle tenure unusually short at about two years, right as the company is financing its largest-ever acquisition (Delivery Hero, $14.8bn) and a multi-year, ~$10bn AV investment program — a moment when finance-leadership continuity matters most, and the record shows the opposite.

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — UBER

**Data-pool caveat (carried from `00_governance-data-triage.md` / `01_management-and-track-record.md`):** no 10-K, 10-Q, or 8-K is present in `data/UBER/`. All financial-statement figures below are Capital IQ vendor transcriptions of Uber's audited FY2021–FY2025 annual filings and FQ2 2026 results (cited as "CIQ export," never as "10-K"), cross-checked against `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`. The Delivery Hero deal terms come from a Capital IQ M&A-database entry (deal-tracker tier, confidence 4) corroborated by the Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript (primary call record) and, for the synergy/leverage figures the pool itself does not state a number for, a dated web source labelled as such. Historical daily/weekly share-price data is not in the pool; buyback average prices below are estimated from quarter-end price marks and labelled as estimates, per the Partial-Data Rule.

**Reporting currency: USD**, except the Delivery Hero deal, which is agreed in EUR and quoted with its own stated USD equivalent — both are shown as filed, not re-derived (CLAUDE.md §27).

## 1. Uses of Capital (5 years, FY2021–FY2025)

| Use | Cumulative Amount | Currency | % of CFO ($21,018mm cumulative FY21–25) | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (net cash paid) | (3,188) | USD mm | 15.2% | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Cash Acquisitions" row, FY2021–FY2025 |
| Buybacks | (7,775) | USD mm | 37.0% | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" row, FY2021–FY2025 |
| Dividends | 0 | USD mm | 0.0% | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Total Dividends Paid" / "Special Dividend Paid" rows, all "-" |
| Capex | (1,351) | USD mm | 6.4% | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Capital Expenditure" row, FY2021–FY2025 |
| Debt repayment (gross) | (10,617) | USD mm | 50.5% | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Total Debt Repaid" row, FY2021–FY2025 |
| *(memo)* Debt issued (gross) | 11,921 | USD mm | 56.7% | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Total Debt Issued" row, FY2021–FY2025 |

Cumulative CFO FY2021–FY2025 = −445 + 642 + 3,585 + 7,137 + 10,099 = **$21,018mm**; cumulative FCF (CFO − |capex|) over the same period = **$19,667mm** [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, §1]. Debt repayment is gross and roughly offset by gross issuance over the period — this reads as refinancing/rollover, not net deleveraging: net debt actually moved from a net-cash position of $(4,050)mm (FY2021) to a small net-debt position of $76mm (FY2025), i.e., broadly flat to modestly worse, before the much larger LTM Jun-2026 jump to $9,340mm described in §3 below [CIQ export — Financials.xls, Balance Sheet tab, "Net Debt" row]. Buybacks did not begin until FY2024 (FY2021–FY2023 buyback rows are all "-"); the 37.0% five-year average understates the recent run rate, which is discussed by year in §3.

**Not shown in this table:** the pending Delivery Hero acquisition ($14.8bn equity value, agreed 2026-07-16) has not closed (expected H2 2027) and is therefore not yet in these cash-flow totals; it is scored on a forward, committed basis in §2 and §4.

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost (divestitures forced / focus lost / options foregone) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Delivery Hero SE (majority stake, ~63.21% additional to a pre-built ~19.5%+5.6% options stake) | Agreed 2026-07-16; expected close H2 2027 | €41.50/share cash; Equity Value €12.9bn ($14.8bn); financed by a committed ~€14bn bridge facility plus cash; termination fee €700mm (Uber) / €200mm (Delivery Hero) | Doubles Uber's addressable delivery footprint to ~100 countries; management states >$1.2bn annualized run-rate synergies targeted within 18 months of close (web-sourced — not itemized in the pool document itself), rooted in migrating Delivery Hero onto Uber's single delivery tech stack, shared payments/cloud infrastructure, and de-duplicated roles; stated to be accretive to non-GAAP EPS at close and "high-single-digit percentage" accretive by year 3 | **Too early to assess — not yet closed.** No integration has begun; the synergy figure is a management target communicated "a couple of weeks ago" [Q2 FY2026 transcript], not a delivered result | As part of the transaction structure, SSW Partners has agreed to acquire Delivery Hero/Uber Eats overlapping businesses in 14 markets for $1.6bn to clear antitrust — a real, disclosed opportunity cost (existing overlap operations given up to complete the deal), not merely a hypothetical one. Uber also pre-funded ~$4bn of the stake via market purchases in Q2 2026, pulled directly from the capital otherwise earmarked for the ~50%-of-FCF buyback framework (see §3) — a direct, quantified trade-off, not a vague "synergy" claim | Capital IQ M&A database (UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf, Delivery Hero SE deal entry, 2026-07-16 / 2026-05-23); Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, CFO remarks, p.11-14; Web: TechCrunch, "Uber's $14.8B Delivery Hero deal would nearly double its global footprint," 2026-07-16 (dated, corroborates the $1.2bn synergy target and <2x gross-leverage target, neither of which is stated verbatim in the pool documents) |
| Trendyol Go (Turkey online meal/grocery delivery) | Closed ~Jun-2025 | Price not disclosed in this pool | Strengthen Turkish delivery position | **Delivered short-term growth, now lapping.** Management states the deal is "rolling off" and being fully lapped in Q3 FY2026, i.e., it inflated FY2025–H1 FY2026 reported Delivery growth and is now becoming a growth headwind on a year-over-year comparison basis — a caution that some of the recent reported Delivery growth was acquired, not organic | Small relative to Uber's balance sheet; opportunity cost not separately quantifiable from this pool | Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, CFO remarks, p.14; UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf, deal entry (Trendyol GO, financial advisor Morgan Stanley, transaction date May-06-2025) |
| Careem Networks (majority-stake reconsolidation) | Original stake acquired 2019–2020 (pre-window); "reconsolidating" per management remarks in FY2026 | Price not disclosed for the reconsolidation step in this pool | Consolidate the MENA "super-app" asset onto Uber's books | **Small, described by management itself as "a smaller acquisition" with modest contribution** — no standalone return/synergy figure disclosed | Not separately quantifiable from this pool | Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, CFO remarks, p.14; UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf |
| Six further bolt-ons in ~18 months: Getir (Türkiye delivery portfolio, pending), SS Ventures/"FlyTaxi" (Hong Kong, closed May-2026), Blacklane GmbH (chauffeur bookings, pending), SpotHero (US/Canada parking marketplace, pending), Segments.ai (AV data-labeling, closed), Crown Taxi (Asia/Pacific, pending) | 2025–2026 | Individual deal prices **not disclosed** in this pool (the CIQ advisor league-table dollar figures next to these entries are each advisor's *cumulative* disclosed deal value across all Uber mandates, not the individual deal's price — attributing them to a single small deal would be a mis-citation) | Each is a small, described-as-strategic bolt-on (geographic footprint or AV-data tooling); no deal-specific synergy or return figure is disclosed | **Not assessable individually — Insufficient Data** on price and return for each; collectively they establish the deal *cadence* (at least seven bolt-ons in ~18 months) that is the basis for the serial-acquirer finding below | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Public Company Profile.rtf, "Current and Pending Subsidiaries/Investments" table; UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf, deal-tracker entries |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** Uber closed or has pending at least seven acquisitions in roughly 18 months, layered under one transformative, debt-funded deal — Delivery Hero at $14.8bn (≈10.6% of Uber's own $139.3bn market cap as of 2026-08-05), financed by a fresh ~€14bn bridge facility. Management's own words on the Q2 FY2026 call — "we tactically pivoted quite heavily towards M&A" and pulled $4bn of capital from the buyback program to build the stake before the deal was even announced — are the opportunity-cost evidence this test asks for, not an inference: capital explicitly promised to buybacks (see §3) was redirected to build a pre-announcement equity stake, and a further $1.6bn of existing overlap businesses must be sold to SSW Partners to clear antitrust. Bare "synergies / strategic fit" language ($1.2bn run-rate, communicated but not yet underwritten by any closed transaction) is not evidence of value creation — it is a target. This is a serial-acquirer pattern per §24 Filter 4, consistent with `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`'s finding (acquisition-pattern severity 78/100, above the ≥70 threshold). **Red Flag RF-CAP-004 triggered — Critical for the M&A-discipline component; caps this scorecard's Capital Allocation total at 50/100 regardless of the underlying component sum (§4).**

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

**This module runs BEFORE valuation; buybacks are judged only on observable trading-range and dilution evidence, not against intrinsic/fair value.** `valuation` module has not run as of this writing — "Intrinsic-value discipline not assessable from this module."

| Year | Amount Spent | Currency | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY2024 | (1,252) | USD mm | Not determinable — pool's quarterly share-price series begins Dec-2024 | **Not determinable from available data** | Shares out rose from 2,076.5mm (Dec-2023) to 2,089.0mm (Dec-2024), **+12.5mm net**, despite the buyback — SBC/option issuance more than offset the FY2024 buyback | **No** — net share count rose this year | Cannot be assessed without an intra-year price series | Buyback started but was smaller than concurrent dilution — a net dilutive year | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab ("Repurchase of Common Stock") and Balance Sheet tab ("Total Shares Out."), FY2023–FY2024 |
| FY2025 (calendar) | (6,523) | USD mm | ≈77mm (estimate: $6,523mm ÷ ~$84.38 average of Mar/Jun/Sep/Dec-2025 quarter-end closing prices — **inference, not from filings**, since no company-disclosed shares-repurchased count or daily VWAP is in the pool) | **≈$84 (estimated, see method above)** | Shares out fell from 2,089.0mm (Dec-2024) to 2,058.1mm (Dec-2025), **−30.9mm net (−1.5%)** | **Partially** — FY2025 buyback ($6,523mm) ran ~3.6x FY2025 stock-based comp expense ($1,826mm) in dollar terms, and net share count did fall, but the fall (−30.9mm) is far smaller than the ~77mm shares the buyback dollars alone would retire at the estimated average price — RSU/option issuance absorbed the rest | The buyback was executed through quarters where the quarter-end price (Jun-2025 $89.22; Sep-2025 $94.67) sat well above the stock's 2026-08-05 close of $69.48 and closer to the 52-week high of $101.99 than the 52-week low of $65.41 — on the trading-range test alone (no valuation judgment implied), FY2025 buybacks were not concentrated at cyclical lows | Net share count reducing = credit; buying disproportionately in the higher part of the range = debit; net verdict **mixed, not clearly disciplined** | Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Financials.xls, Historical Capitalization tab (quarterly share price/shares out, Dec-2024 to Mar-2026); Public Company Profile.rtf (52wk high/low, spot price); CIQ export, Cash Flow tab ("Stock-Based Compensation") |
| LTM ended Jun-2026 | (6,904) | USD mm | ≈87mm (estimate: $6,904mm ÷ ~$78.96 average of Sep-2025/Dec-2025/Mar-2026 quarter-end prices and the Aug-2026 spot as a Jun-2026 proxy — **inference, not from filings**) | **≈$79 (estimated)** | Shares out fell from 2,077.8mm (Sep-2025) to 2,035.6mm (Mar-2026), then rose to 2,042.6mm by the Aug-2026 quote — the buyback pause described below shows up directly as a share-count uptick | **Partially**, same reasoning as FY2025 row; ratio to LTM SBC ($1,939mm) ≈3.6x | Buybacks ran through Sep-2025 ($94.67 quarter-end) before the stock fell to $69.99 by Dec-2025 — again, buying occurred disproportionately before the price decline, not after it | Same mixed verdict, compounded by the framework override below | Same as FY2025 row, plus Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript |
| Q2 FY2026 (component of LTM, called out separately) | ~(3,500) YTD through Jun-2026 per management, of which **$4,000mm was redirected to Delivery Hero equity-stake purchases** (booked as "Invest. in Marketable & Equity Securt.," not as a share repurchase) rather than to buybacks | USD mm | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | Management's own framework is "about 50% of free cash flow" to buybacks; against ~$10.1bn trailing FCF that implies ~$5.0–5.1bn/year, and the company states it ran **above** that rate through FY2024–FY2025 before abruptly pulling $4bn away in Q2 FY2026 for the Delivery Hero stake — a standing capital-return promise overridden without a stated pause target when the M&A opportunity arose | **RF-CAP-002 test: buybacks ARE net share-count reducing (not merely offsetting SBC) over FY2024–Mar2026, so RF-CAP-002 is NOT triggered** on the share-count test alone; the framework-override behavior is a candor/consistency flag captured in `01-017`, not a share-count red flag | Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, CFO remarks, p.12; CIQ export, Cash Flow tab, "Invest. in Marketable & Equity Securt." row (LTM −4,772 vs FY2025 −2,077) |

**Net read on buybacks:** across FY2024–LTM Jun-2026, Uber spent $14,679mm cumulatively on buybacks against $3,765mm of stock-based compensation expense over the same window (≈3.9x), and net share count fell modestly (2,089.0mm Dec-2024 → 2,042.6mm Aug-2026, −2.2%). Buybacks are net share-count reducing, which is the minimum bar; they are not evidence of price discipline on the trading-range test, since the heaviest buying quarters (mid-to-late 2025) sit well above where the stock trades today.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Payout ratio | 0% — Uber has never paid a common or special dividend in any period shown (FY2021–LTM Jun-2026) | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Total Dividends Paid" / "Special Dividend Paid," all periods "-" |
| Dividend coverage (FCF) | Not applicable — no dividend to cover | Same as above |
| Incremental ROIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital) | **≈35% over FY2022→FY2025** (inference — approximation, not company-disclosed segment ROIC): Δ EBIT = $5,565mm − $(1,832)mm = $7,397mm; applying a 21% statutory-rate proxy for NOPAT (Uber's own effective GAAP tax rate is distorted by one-time deferred-tax benefits — see `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §4 — so a normalized statutory rate is used instead) gives ΔNOPAT ≈ $5,844mm; invested capital (total debt + total equity − cash & ST investments) rose from $15,910mm (FY2022) to $32,752mm (FY2025), ΔIC = $16,842mm; $5,844mm / $16,842mm ≈ 34.7%. A shorter FY2023→FY2025 window gives ≈25.5% (ΔNOPAT $3,519mm / ΔIC $13,775mm). Both readings sit well above a reasonable cost of capital for this business (mid-to-high single digits to low double digits), and both are **organic** — capex over the same period never exceeded $336mm/year against $737–774mm/year of D&A, so the operating turnaround was not bought with heavy reinvestment | CIQ export — Financials.xls, Income Statement tab (EBIT, FY2022/FY2023/FY2025), Balance Sheet tab (Total Equity, Total Debt, Cash & ST Investments, FY2022/FY2023/FY2025) |

Uber's capital-return framework substitutes buybacks for dividends entirely; given the capital already committed to the AV investment program (~$10bn multi-year, per `01_management-and-track-record.md`) and the pending Delivery Hero financing, a policy of retaining/buying back rather than paying a dividend is a defensible structural choice for this stage of the business — not itself a governance negative — but it also means shareholders have no cash floor if the M&A and AV bets underperform.

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Judged on what management actually did with cash, not what it said it would do: the **organic** business is a genuine per-share value creator — Uber turned EBIT from a $(1,832)mm loss (FY2022) to $5,565mm of profit (FY2025) on capex that never exceeded $336mm/year, an incremental ROIC in the 25–35% range by this module's approximation, comfortably clearing its cost of capital. Buybacks reduced net share count modestly (−2.2% since Dec-2024) and ran ahead of stock-based-comp dilution in dollar terms (≈3.9x), which clears the minimum bar of "not merely offsetting SBC" — but the heaviest buying quarters were priced well above where the stock trades today, so this was not price-disciplined buying by the observable trading-range test. The single biggest capital-allocation signal, and the worst use of capital on the evidence available, is the M&A program: at least seven bolt-on deals in eighteen months culminating in a $14.8bn, debt-funded acquisition of Delivery Hero — financed by a fresh ~€14bn bridge facility, requiring a forced $1.6bn divestiture of existing overlap businesses to clear antitrust, and funded in part by diverting $4bn away from the buyback program the company had promised shareholders. The synergies management cites ($1.2bn run-rate) are a target communicated weeks before this report, not a delivered result, and the deal will not even close until H2 2027. **Verdict: Mixed** — organic capital allocation is value-creative and buybacks are (barely) per-share accretive on a share-count basis, but the M&A pattern is the dominant, least-proven, and highest-risk capital decision management has made, and it is debt-funded at a moment the balance sheet had only just rebuilt itself toward net cash.

## management-governance / 03_incentives-and-compensation.md

# Incentives & Compensation — UBER

**Data-pool caveat (carried from `00_governance-data-triage.md` and `01_management-and-track-record.md`):** no DEF 14A (proxy statement), 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, or shareholder letter is present anywhere in `data/UBER/`. The pool is a Capital IQ vendor-export set (company profile, board/professionals bios, financial-statement workbooks, an estimates/consensus workbook) plus one Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript (Aug-05-2026). **None of these documents contains a Compensation Discussion & Analysis, a Summary Compensation Table, bonus/LTIP plan design, performance-metric weights, or a say-on-pay vote result for Uber.** Per this agent's partial-data rule, incentive alignment **cannot be assessed** from primary evidence. This report states that plainly, extracts the one high-level, pool-verifiable fact available (aggregate stock-based compensation, company-wide), and uses the web only for peer-comparator context (Lyft, DoorDash bonus/LTIP design), clearly labeled and dated — it does not use the web to fill in Uber's own metric weights, which would not be a pool-verified fact and is explicitly barred by this agent's operating rule.

## 1. Compensation Structure

| Executive | Base | Annual Bonus | Long-Term Incentive | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| CEO (Dara Khosrowshahi) | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | No DEF 14A / CD&A / Summary Compensation Table in pool |
| CFO (Balaji Krishnamurthy, CFA — in role since 2026-02-16) | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | No DEF 14A / CD&A / Summary Compensation Table in pool |

Reporting currency: USD (company reports in USD; see `01_management-and-track-record.md`). Period: not assessable — no compensation period (FY2025 or FY2026) is disclosed in any pool document.

**The one pool-verifiable, company-wide (not executive-specific) data point:** stock-based compensation expense (all employees, before tax) was $1,796mm in FY2024, $1,826mm in FY2025, and $1,939mm on an LTM basis to Jun-30-2026 [Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Stock-Based Compensation" row; Supplemental tab, "Stock Based Comp. Exp., Before Tax" row]. On LTM revenue of $55,227mm (per `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`), that is roughly 3.3% of LTM revenue — directionally confirms a broadly equity-heavy pay culture at Uber, but this is an aggregate accounting line, not a CEO/CFO-specific compensation figure, and cannot be decomposed into base/bonus/LTIP by executive from the pool.

**Peer context (web-sourced, unverified, dated 2026-08-06 — for texture only, not a substitute for Uber's own proxy):** as one data point on rideshare/delivery-platform CEO pay design generally, Lyft's FY2025 DEF 14A (per third-party proxy summaries) describes CEO David Risher's base salary at $725,000 with an annual cash bonus tied 50%/50% to Gross Bookings and Adjusted EBITDA, both size/scale metrics rather than per-share or returns metrics [Web: Lyft FY2025 DEF 14A summary, accessed 2026-08-06, unverified]. DoorDash's CEO Tony Xu, by contrast, draws a nominal ~$300,000 salary with no cash bonus and effectively all upside from a 2020 "CEO Performance Award" that vests only if DoorDash's stock price clears a ladder of absolute price hurdles over seven years — a purely per-share/TSR-linked design [Web: multiple third-party compensation trackers (salary.com, AFL-CIO Paywatch) summarizing DoorDash's 2020 CEO Performance Award and FY2025 Summary Compensation Table, accessed 2026-08-06, unverified]. These two peers bracket the design space (size-metric annual bonus vs. pure stock-price LTIP) but say nothing about which design Uber itself uses.

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Annual bonus | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | Not assessable | No CD&A / bonus-plan disclosure in pool |
| Long-term incentive | Insufficient Data | Insufficient Data | Not assessable | No LTIP-plan disclosure in pool |

No bonus or LTIP metric, weight, or target for Uber's own named executive officers is disclosed in any document in `data/UBER/`. This module does not infer or fabricate weights from the peer data above (§2 of this agent's rules explicitly bars that). The only related, disclosed fact is a **company-level capital-allocation commitment**, not a compensation-plan mechanism: management states a policy of returning "about 50% of free cash flow" to share buybacks [Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, CFO remarks, p.12, cited in `01_management-and-track-record.md` §3]. That is a capital-return policy, not evidence of what the CEO/CFO bonus or LTIP actually pays on — it is noted here only because a per-share-oriented capital policy is one of the few available directional signals on management's stated orientation, and it is kept clearly separate from an actual pay-metric finding.

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest) | Insufficient Data — no Summary Compensation Table for FY2025 or FY2026 anywhere in the pool | Not disclosed in `data/UBER/` |
| Pay vs peers (if available) | Not assessable for Uber. Peer design texture only (Lyft: size-metric bonus; DoorDash: pure stock-price LTIP) shown in §1, web-sourced and unverified | Web sources, accessed 2026-08-06 (peer-benchmarking use only, per this agent's operating rule) |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | Not assessable — no pay figures and no performance-metric disclosure exist in the pool to compare | Not disclosed in `data/UBER/` |
| Say-on-pay support (%) | Insufficient Data. US issuers hold an annual non-binding say-on-pay vote as a matter of SEC/Dodd-Frank requirement (a structural, jurisdiction-level fact, not Uber-specific content), but the actual FY2026 vote-support percentage is not in the pool and this agent does not extract it from Uber's own proxy per the partial-data restriction on web use | Structural fact only; specific vote % not sourced |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

The honest answer is that incentive alignment **cannot be assessed** for Uber from the data available: there is no proxy statement, no Compensation Discussion & Analysis, no Summary Compensation Table, and no bonus/LTIP metric or weight disclosure anywhere in `data/UBER/`, so neither the size of CEO/CFO pay nor what it actually rewards can be verified. The only pool-anchored signal — aggregate, company-wide stock-based compensation running ~3.3% of LTM revenue [Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Financials.xls, Cash Flow / Supplemental tabs] alongside a disclosed policy of directing roughly half of free cash flow to buybacks [Q2 FY2026 earnings-call transcript, CFO remarks] — points toward a broadly equity-funded, per-share-conscious capital posture at the company level, but it says nothing about how the CEO and CFO are personally scored or paid, and it must not be read as a substitute finding for the missing executive-level disclosure. Conclusion: **not assessable — insufficient data**, and the single highest-value next input is Uber's own DEF 14A (Compensation Discussion & Analysis, Summary Compensation Table, Grants of Plan-Based Awards, and the say-on-pay vote result).

## management-governance / 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — UBER

**Data-pool caveat.** No DEF 14A, Schedule 13D/13G, or Form 4 filing is present anywhere in `data/UBER/`. Ownership and insider-transaction data instead come from S&P Capital IQ's beneficial-ownership tables and "Insider Trading Activity Summary" inside `UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf` (report-generation date Aug-05-2026), cross-checked against `Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Board Members.rtf` and `Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Public Company Profile.rtf`. This is a **pool-sourced vendor filing** — Capital IQ / Bloomberg sits at source-hierarchy tier 5 (CLAUDE.md §4) and is not itself a Schedule 13D/13G or Form 4, so red flags below are gated to this pool document per fix F20 rather than to any web/memory figure. The transaction-level insider-trade detail in the pool covers only the trailing **6 months** (Feb-2026 through Jul-2026), not the full 12 months this report's template calls for; the 7–12-month-back window (Aug-2025–Jan-2026) is not covered by any document in this pool and is marked "Insufficient Data" rather than filled in from memory. Ownership (a static %) and insider transactions (the behavior signal) are reported separately below, per MODULE_RULES.md.

## 1. Ownership

| CEO (non-founder) — Dara Khosrowshahi | 0.060% (1,225,802 shares, $87.8mm) | Single class common (NYSE:UBER) | Overwhelmingly stock-compensation-derived (RSU vesting), not open-market cash purchase — see §2 | `UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf`, "Top 25 Holders (Individuals/Insiders)" |
| Other insiders (aggregate — all remaining officers & directors) | 0.116% (2,360,305 shares, ~$169.0mm) | Single class common | Same pattern — grant/vest-driven, not cash-funded | `UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf`, "Top 25 Holders (Individuals/Insiders)" |
| **Total Individuals/Insiders** | **0.18%** (3,586,107 shares, $256.8mm; precise figure 0.176%) | Single class common | Grant/vest-driven — see §2 | `UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf`, "Public Ownership Summary" and "Top 25 Holders (Individuals/Insiders)" |
| Largest institutional holder — BlackRock, Inc. | 7.417% (150,987,293 shares, $10,812.2mm) | Single class common | N/A — index/active asset manager, not an insider | `UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf`, "Top 25 Holders" |

Alnowaiser, PIF's Deputy Governor & Head of International Investment, on the board since Nov-16-2023, Audit Committee member) | Single class common | N/A — passive/strategic institutional stake, not an operating insider | `UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf`, "Top 25 Holders"; `Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Board Members.rtf`, Alnowaiser bio |
| Promoter / controlling bloc | **None** — Uber has no promoter, founder-control group, or dual-class structure. Co-founder Garrett Camp is a non-voting Board Observer only, with no listed equity stake in the Top 25 Individuals/Insiders table | Single class common; Float 99.7% | N/A | `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, disqualifier #2; `Uber Technologies Inc NYSE UBER Public Company Profile.rtf`, "Float %" |

**Total insider (officers + directors) ownership: 0.18%** of the 2,042,560,121 shares outstanding — a negligible stake for a company this size. Institutions hold 84.00% of shares outstanding, corporations (public+private) 0.03%, individuals/insiders 0.18%, state-owned shares (a separate small CIQ classification, distinct from PIF's institutional-bucket stake) 0.12%, and the balance is public/other float [`UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape.rtf`, "Public Ownership Summary"]. This is a widely-held, professionally-managed register, not an owner-operator structure — consistent with `01_management-and-track-record.md`'s finding that Khosrowshahi is a hired-in CEO (not a founder) with no meaningful bought stake.

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months — pool covers only the trailing 6 months; see caveat above)

|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Dara Khosrowshahi, CEO & Director | "Purchase" (RSU vest, $0 value) | 243,258 shares | Mar-16-2026 | No cash paid — a stock-compensation vesting event, not an open-market buy | CIQ Landscape, "Recent Insider Purchases (Last 6 Months)" |
| Dara Khosrowshahi, CEO & Director | Sale | (253,700) shares, $(18.94mm) at $74.66 | Mar-16-2026 | Same-day sell of vested shares (sell-to-cover pattern); recurs on a monthly "16th-of-the-month" cycle across multiple executives, consistent with a pre-set Rule 10b5-1 program — **Inference, not from filings**, as the pool does not explicitly label these as 10b5-1 | CIQ Landscape, "Recent Insider Sales (Last 6 Months)" |
| Balaji Krishnamurthy, CFO | Purchase (only transaction in the 6-month window with a non-zero disclosed value) | 22,453 shares, $1.60mm at $71.25–71.32 | Feb-24-2026 | Ambiguous in this data — could be an option exercise (paying the strike price) rather than a discretionary open-market cash buy; the pool does not disclose the transaction mechanism, so this is **not** treated as a conviction open-market purchase | CIQ Landscape, "Recent Insider Purchases (Last 6 Months)" |

Affairs), Andrew MacDonald (President & COO) | Recurring monthly "purchase" (RSU vest, $0 value) immediately followed by "sale" at market price on the same date | ~1,800–6,400 shares vested and ~1,800–5,700 shares sold per person, per month, Feb-2026 through Jul-2026 | 16th of each month, Feb–Jul 2026 | Near-1:1 match between vest count and same-day sale count for each name/date — classic sell-to-cover pattern, not discretionary buying or selling | CIQ Landscape, "Recent Insider Purchases" and "Recent Insider Sales (Last 6 Months)" |
| Nikki Krishnamurthy (Former Chief People Officer), Prashanth Mahendra-Rajah (Former CFO) | Vest + same-day sale, then departed the roster ("Former") | Small share counts, $0-value vests / market-price sales | Feb–Apr 2026 | Departing-executive vesting/settlement activity, coincides with the CFO transition already flagged in `01_management-and-track-record.md` | CIQ Landscape, same tables |
| Independent directors (Thain, Sugar, Wynaendts, Alnowaiser, Trujillo) | Small RSU-style grants (purchase, $0 value); two directors (Wynaendts, Alnowaiser) also sold small amounts at $75.12 | 300–5,400 shares per grant/sale | May-2026 (AGM-cycle grant date) | Standard annual director equity grant/vest, not open-market buying | CIQ Landscape, same tables |

**Net insider activity, last 6 months:** 44 purchase transactions / 574,497 shares vs. 39 sale transactions / 608,470 shares → **net sales of 33,973 shares**. **Last 3 months:** 16 purchases / 51,482 shares vs. 15 sales / 55,091 shares → **net sales of 3,609 shares** [CIQ Landscape, "Insider Trading Activity Summary"]. Read plainly: this is **net selling, not net buying**, but almost all of it is mechanical — routine RSU-vest-then-sell-to-cover-taxes on a recurring monthly schedule across multiple named executives, not a burst of discretionary open-market disposals. Across the entire 6-month window, only one transaction (Krishnamurthy's Feb-24-2026 purchase) carries a disclosed non-zero purchase value, and even that one is not clearly identifiable as an open-market cash buy rather than an option exercise. **No insider in this pool bought stock with their own cash in a way that reads as a conviction signal.** Distinguishing the categories the report asks for: open-market discretionary buys = none identified; option exercises = one ambiguous instance (Krishnamurthy, Feb-24-2026); scheduled/10b5-1-style sell-to-cover sales = the large majority of the activity (Inference on the 10b5-1 label, not confirmed by a filing).
